{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{72.9792pt}{-3.40334pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(3.29)

The lack of factorisation of the star-commutator
[-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}-]_{\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})}
, discussed in §
3.1
, means that
we cannot simply strip off the rigid
\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})
symmetry, like we did with
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}
in the biadjoint theory from §
2.2
. Instead, we show that the infinite-dimensional Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})
admits a ‘twisted factorisation’, with the twist implementing the intertwining of colour and kinematical degrees of freedom of the scalar fields.

For this, we factorize
\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star}
into three parts: a colour part, a twisted colour part and a cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra which fully describes the trivalent interactions. There are two steps: In the first step, we show that
\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star}
admits a factorization into the colour Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and a kinematical cyclic strict
C_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}
, which corresponds to colour-stripping the cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star}
.

The cochain complex underlying
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}
is

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ch}(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})})=\Big(\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N}))[-1]\xrightarrow{\ \square\ }\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N}))[-2]\Big)\ ,

(3.30)

which again identifies the differential
{m}_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}=\square
acting as
{m}_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}(\phi)=\square\,\phi_{\bar{a}}\,\bar{T}^{\bar{a}}
.
The product
m_{2}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}:\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N}))[-1]^{\otimes 2}\longrightarrow\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N}))[-2]
is defined by

\displaystyle m_{2}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=-g\,[\phi_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\phi_{2}]_{\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})}\ .

(3.31)

This operation is clearly graded commutative, and for degree reasons it satisfies the Leibniz rule as well as the graded associativity relation. The cyclic structure is defined by
\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}=\int\,\circ\,\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})}
.

This cyclic strict
C_{\infty}
-algebra allows for a factorization

\displaystyle\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star}=\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{C}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}\ .

(3.32)

For this, we identify the vector space
\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N}))
in the usual way with the tensor product
\mathfrak{g}\otimes\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N}))
, recall the definition of the differential
\mu_{1}^{\star{\textrm{\tiny BAS}}}
and the
2
-bracket
\mu_{2}^{\star{\textrm{\tiny BAS}}}
, and use
eq.

3.19
as well as
eq.

3.21
. It then easily follows that the structure maps of
\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star}
factor through
\mu_{1}^{\star\textrm{\tiny BAS}}=\mathbbm{1}\otimes m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}
,
\mu_{2}^{\star\textrm{\tiny BAS}}=[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes m_{2}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}
and
\langle-,-\rangle_{\textrm{\tiny BAS}}^{\star}=\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}
.

The second step consists in stripping off the kinematical factor encoding the trivalent interactions. This uses the notion of twisted tensor product of homotopy algebras from
[
12
]
,
5
5

5

The general notion of ‘twist’ in this context should not be confused with Drinfel’d twist deformation in the context of noncommutative geometry, though in our setting they both play a similar role in defining a noncommutative deformation.
which we review in §
A.2
. Regarding
\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})
as a graded vector space sitting in degree
0
, we wish to compute the twisted tensor product between
\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})
and the cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{Scal}
of the commutative
g\,\phi^{3}
-theory on
\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1}
from §
2.1
. The twist datum
\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}=\big(\tau_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})},\tau_{2}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}\big)
consists of a pair of maps
\tau_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}:\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\otimes\mathsf{End}(L)
and
\tau_{2}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}:\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\otimes\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\longrightarrow\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\otimes\mathsf{End}(L)\otimes\mathsf{End}(L)
, where

\displaystyle L=\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1})[-1]\oplus\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1})[-2]

(3.33)

is the graded vector space underlying the cochain complex
\mathsf{Ch}_{\rm Scal}
from
eq.

2.4
.

We set

\displaystyle\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}_{1}(\bar{T}^{\bar{a}})=\bar{T}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}

(3.34)

and

\displaystyle\tau_{2}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}(\bar{T}^{\bar{a}},\bar{T}^{\bar{b}})=[\bar{T}^{\bar{a}},\bar{T}^{\bar{b}}]_{\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})}\otimes\cos\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,\theta^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\otimes\partial_{\nu}\big)+\mathrm{i}\,\{\bar{T}^{\bar{a}},\bar{T}^{\bar{b}}\}_{\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})}\otimes\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,\theta^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\otimes\partial_{\nu}\big)\ .

(3.35)

Following the prescription of
[
12
]
, the tensor product
\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\otimes L
now carries the structure of a cyclic strict
C_{\infty}
-algebra with differential

\displaystyle m_{1}^{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}}(\bar{T}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\phi_{\bar{a}})=\bar{T}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\mu_{1}^{\rm Scal}(\phi_{\bar{a}})=\bar{T}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\square\,\phi_{\bar{a}}\ ,

(3.36)

and product

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{2}^{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}}(\bar{T}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\phi^{\bar{a}}_{1},\bar{T}^{\bar{b}}\otimes\phi^{\bar{b}}_{2})&=[\bar{T}^{\bar{a}},\bar{T}^{\bar{b}}]_{\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})}\otimes\mu_{2}^{\rm Scal}\circ\cos\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,\theta^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\otimes\partial_{\nu}\big)(\phi_{1}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\phi_{2}^{\bar{b}})\\
&\quad\,+\mathrm{i}\,\{\bar{T}^{\bar{a}},\bar{T}^{\bar{b}}\}_{\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})}\otimes\mu_{2}^{\rm Scal}\circ\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,\theta^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\otimes\partial_{\nu}\big)(\phi_{1}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\phi_{2}^{\bar{b}})\\[4.0pt]
&=-g\,[\bar{T}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\phi^{\bar{a}}_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\bar{T}^{\bar{b}}\otimes\phi^{\bar{b}}_{2}]_{\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})}\ ,\end{split}

(3.37)

where we used
eqs.

A.38d
and
2.5
. The cyclic structure does not twist.
This cyclic strict
C_{\infty}
-algebra is the twisted tensor product of
\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})
and
\mathfrak{Scal}
, denoted
\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}}\mathfrak{Scal}
. From the definitions it immediately follows that
m_{1}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}=m_{1}^{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}}
and
m_{2}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}=m_{2}^{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}}
, and hence the cyclic strict
C_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}
factorizes as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}=\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}}\mathfrak{Scal}\ .

(3.38)

Altogether, we have shown that the cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra of the noncommutative biadjoint scalar theory on
\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1}
admits the factorization

\displaystyle\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star}=\mathfrak{g}\otimes\big(\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}}\mathfrak{Scal}\big)\ .

(3.39)

This is one of the main messages of this paper: colour-stripping is achievable in noncommutative field theories through a suitable notion of twisted factorisation, which enables one to disentangle colour and kinematical degrees of freedom. From this perspective noncommutativity is completely absorbed into the twist maps
\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(\bar{N})}
. This will also be applicable to the noncommutative theories with local gauge symmetry that we consider later on.

3.4
Adjoint scalar theory

The limiting rank one case
\bar{N}=1
of the noncommutative biadjoint scalar theory is an interesting noncommutative scalar field theory that does not have a non-trivial commutative counterpart, in the sense that it becomes non-interacting in the commutative limit. We therefore refrain from using the adjective ‘noncommutative’ and simply refer to it as the adjoint scalar theory on
\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1}
. We begin by briefly summarising its main features, which follow by setting
\bar{N}=1
everywhere in §
3.2
.

The cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{AS}
of this theory has cochain complex

\displaystyle\mathsf{Ch}(\mathfrak{AS})=\Big(\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{g})[-1]\xrightarrow{\ \square\ }\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{g})[-2]\Big)\ ,

(3.40)

and the single non-vanishing higher bracket

\displaystyle\mu_{2}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})=-g\,[\![T^{a}\otimes\phi_{1}^{a},T^{b}\otimes\phi_{2}^{b}]\!]_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}=-\mathrm{i}\,g\,[T^{a},T^{b}]_{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes[\phi_{1}^{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\phi_{2}^{b}]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\ ,

(3.41)

where we abbreviate
\llbracket-,-\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}:=\llbracket-,-\rrbracket_{\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}
.
Note that this binary operation is not the antisymmetric Lie bracket
[\phi_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\phi_{2}]_{\mathfrak{g}}
; in particular, it vanishes in the commutative limit
t=0
. The cyclic structure is given by

\displaystyle\langle\phi,\phi^{+}\rangle_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}=\int\,\mathrm{d}^{d}x\ \mathrm{Tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\phi\star\phi^{+})=\int\,\mathrm{d}^{d}x\ \phi^{a}\star\phi_{a}^{+}\ .

(3.42)

The equations of motion

\displaystyle\square\,\phi^{a}-\mathrm{i}\,g\,f^{abc}\,\phi^{b}\star\phi^{c}=0

(3.43)

of the adjoint scalar theory are the variational equations of the action functional

\displaystyle\begin{split}S_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}[\phi]&=\int\,\mathrm{d}^{d}x\ \frac{1}{2}\,\phi^{a}\star\square\,\phi_{a}-\frac{\mathrm{i}\,g}{3}\,f^{abc}\,\phi^{a}\star\phi^{b}\star\phi^{c}\ .\end{split}

(3.44)

The three-point Feynman vertex defined by
eq.

3.44
is

\begin{split}\hbox to212.1pt{\vbox to75.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 36.83588pt\lower-38.78566pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

Remark 3.46
.

The adjoint scalar theory is formally obtained from the standard biadjoint scalar theory of §
2.2
by replacing the second Lie algebra
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}
with the kinematical Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)
, via the prescription

\displaystyle\phi^{a\bar{a}}\longrightarrow\phi^{a}\ ,\quad\bar{f}^{\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{c}}\longrightarrow\bar{F}(k,p,q)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathfrak{g}\otimes\bar{\mathfrak{g}}}\longrightarrow\textrm{\small$\int$}\,\circ\,\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathfrak{g}}

(3.47)

in the Lagrangian of the commutative biadjoint scalar theory. This line of reasoning was used in
[
28
]
to discuss aspects of a non-perturbative Lagrangian-level double copy between these field theories.

Twisted factorisation.

The factorisation of the adjoint scalar
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{AS}
follows from the
\bar{N}=1
limit of the twisted factorisation (
3.39
), but with a crucial simplification. We treat the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)
as a kinematical factor by identifying
\mathfrak{u}(1)\simeq\mathbbm{R}
and regarding it as the kinematical vector space of the adjoint scalar theory:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Kin}_{\wedge^{0}}:=\mathbbm{R}\ .

(3.48)

The tensor products (over
\mathbbm{R}
) with the one-dimensional vector space
\mathbbm{R}
are trivial, so that we may identify
\mathbbm{R}\otimes W\simeq W
for any real vector space
W
. The definition of the twist datum
\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}
correspondingly simplifies to

\displaystyle\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}_{1}(1)=\mathbbm{1}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\tau_{2}^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}(1)=2\,\mathrm{i}\,\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,\theta^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\otimes\partial_{\nu}\big)\ ,

(3.49)

from which one recovers the star-commutator bracket
[-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}-]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}
.

Thus the cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra of the adjoint scalar theory on
\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1}
factorises as

\displaystyle\mathfrak{AS}=\mathfrak{g}\otimes(\mathfrak{Kin}_{\wedge^{0}}\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}}\mathfrak{Scal})\ .

(3.50)

This agrees with the prescription in (
3.47
), and it identifies the commutative biadjoint scalar theory as the zeroth copy of the adjoint scalar theory: replacing the kinematic factor
\mathfrak{Kin}_{\wedge^{0}}=\mathbbm{R}
in the factorisation (
3.50
) with the second colour factor
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}
yields the cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra (
2.21
),
which is the correct homotopy algebraic structure in the zeroth copy prescription.

3.5
Colour-kinematics duality

In terms of the factorization (
3.50
), the double copy prescription for the adjoint scalar theory is immediately apparent.
However, we must first verify that the factorisation is compatible with colour-kinematics duality.
To do this, we will demonstrate how colour-kinematics duality is manifest in the Berends–Giele currents of the adjoint scalar theory. This framework realises the double copy as a generalisation of the colour-kinematics dual formulation (relying on kinematic Jacobi identities), rather than the KLT formulation (relying on relations amongst colour ordered amplitudes) that we will encounter in
section

5
.

Perturbative calculations.

We first give the perturbiner expansion, reviewed in §
A.3
, for the adjoint scalar theory.
The contracting homotopy satisfying the Hodge–Kodaira decomposition for the projection to the minimal model of the
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{AS}
is simply the massless Feynman propagator

\begin{split}(\mu_{1}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\circ G_{\textrm{F}})(x,y)=\mathrm{i}\,\delta(x-y)\qquad\mbox{with}\quad G_{\textrm{F}}(\mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p\cdot x})=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p^{2}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p\cdot x}\ ,\end{split}

(3.51)

where
\mu_{1}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}=\square
.
From a perturbiner element
\phi^{\circ}\in{\sf H}^{1}(\mathfrak{AS})=\ker(\square)
, we look for a quasi-isomorphism
\psi_{n}:{\sf H}^{1}(\mathfrak{AS})^{\otimes n}\longrightarrow L^{1}
which is given by the recursion relations (
A.37b
).
Given
\phi^{\circ}
, a perturbiner expansion for the interacting theory is nothing but the Maurer–Cartan field under this quasi-isomorphism, namely

\phi=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\,\frac{1}{n!}\,\psi_{n}(\phi^{\circ\,\otimes n})\ \in\ \Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{g})[-1]\ .

(3.52)

We now consider the decomposition in terms of on-shell multiparticle solutions. This differs from the formula (
3.52
) that is used to reconstruct a Maurer–Cartan element in the full interacting theory from identical free fields. Denote by
\phi(i)\in{\sf H}^{1}(\mathfrak{AS})
a field in the minimal model corresponding to the ‘
i
-th’ adjoint scalar.
Start by decomposing the quasi-isomorphism maps in terms of plane waves with coefficients
J(1\cdots n)
, called the Berends–Giele currents:

\psi_{n}\big(\phi(1),\ldots,\phi(n)\big)=J(1\cdots n)\ \mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p_{1\cdots n}\cdot x}\ \in\ \Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{g})[-1]\ ,

(3.53)

where
p_{1\cdots n}:=p_{1}+\cdots+p_{n}
.
These currents are sometimes written as
\phi_{1\cdots n}
; they are simply the Fourier coefficients of the quasi-isomorphism applied to
n
different external states.

The recursion relations for the currents are extracted from the quasi-isomorphisms
\psi_{n}
, and one finds for
n>1

\begin{split}J(1\cdots n)&=\frac{g}{2}\,\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\ \sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(i;n)}\,G_{\rm F}\circ\llbracket J(\sigma(1),\ldots,\sigma(i)),J(\sigma(i+1),\ldots,\sigma(n))\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\ ,\end{split}

(3.54)

where we understand
G_{\rm F}
as acting on the plane wave basis representing the double bracket.
Writing
\phi(i)=\phi_{i}\,\mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p_{i}\cdot x}
for
\phi_{i}\in\Omega^{0}(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{g})
, the first few currents are given by

\begin{split}J(1)&=\phi_{1}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
J(12)&=-\mathrm{i}\,g\,\frac{\llbracket\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}}{s_{12}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
J(123)&=-(\,\mathrm{i}\,g)^{2}\,\bigg(\frac{\llbracket\phi_{1},\llbracket\phi_{2},\phi_{3}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}}{s_{23}\,s_{123}}+\frac{\llbracket\phi_{2},\llbracket\phi_{3},\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}}{s_{31}\,s_{123}}+\frac{\llbracket\phi_{3},\llbracket\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}}{s_{12}\,s_{123}}\bigg)\ ,\end{split}

(3.55)

and so on, where
s_{ij\cdots}=(p_{i}+p_{j}+\cdots)^{2}
are Mandelstam invariants.
This verifies that the currents are cyclic symmetric.

The brackets on the minimal model, for the projection
{\sf p}:L^{1}\longrightarrow{\sf H}^{1}(\mathfrak{AS})
, are given by
eq.

A.37d
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mu_{n}^{\circ{\textrm{\tiny AS}}}\big(\phi(1),\ldots,\phi(n)\big)\\[4.0pt]
&\hskip 56.9055pt=-\frac{g}{2}\,\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\ \sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(i;n)}\,{\sf p}\circ\llbracket J(\sigma(1),\ldots,\sigma(i)),J(\sigma(i+1),\ldots,\sigma(n))\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\ \mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p_{1\cdots n}\cdot x}\ .\end{split}

(3.56)

For the first few brackets this gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mu^{\circ\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{2}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})&=-g\,\llbracket\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\ \mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p_{12}\cdot x}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\mu^{\circ\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{3}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3})&=\mathrm{i}\,g^{2}\,\bigg(\frac{\llbracket\phi_{1},\llbracket\phi_{2},\phi_{3}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}}{s_{23}}+\frac{\llbracket\phi_{2},\llbracket\phi_{3},\phi_{1}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}}{s_{31}}\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt+\frac{\llbracket\phi_{3},\llbracket\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}}{s_{12}}\bigg)\ \mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p_{123}\cdot x}\ ,\end{split}

(3.57)

and so on.

Ordered tree-level amplitudes are given by the cyclic structure of the minimal model, taken on distinct perturbiner elements as in
eq.

A.41
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{n}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}(1,\dots,n)=\langle\phi_{1},\mu_{n-1}^{\circ\textrm{\tiny AS}}(\phi_{2},\dots,\phi_{n})\rangle_{\circ\textrm{\tiny AS}}\ .

(3.58)

The full amplitude
\mathscr{A}_{n}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}(p,a)
is given by the sum over all planar ordered permutations (cf.
section

2.2
).

For example, the full tree-level off-shell four-point amplitude is given by a sum of three terms

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{4}(p,a)=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(1;3)}\,\mathcal{M}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{4}\big(1,\sigma(2),\sigma(3),\sigma(4)\big)=\frac{c_{s}\,n_{s}}{s}+\frac{c_{t}\,n_{t}}{t}+\frac{c_{u}\,n_{u}}{u}\ ,

(3.59)

where the kinematical numerators are

\displaystyle\begin{split}n_{s}&=g\,\int\,\mathrm{d}^{d}q\ \bar{F}(p_{1},p_{2},q)\,\bar{F}(q,p_{3},p_{4})=-4\,g\,\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,p_{1}\cdot\theta\,p_{2}\big)\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,p_{3}\cdot\theta\,p_{4}\big)\ ,\\[4.0pt]
n_{t}&=g\,\int\,\mathrm{d}^{d}q\ \bar{F}(p_{3},p_{1},q)\,\bar{F}(q,p_{2},p_{4})=-4\,g\,\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,p_{3}\cdot\theta\,p_{1}\big)\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,p_{2}\cdot\theta\,p_{4}\big)\ ,\\[4.0pt]
n_{u}&=g\,\int\,\mathrm{d}^{d}q\ \bar{F}(p_{2},p_{3},q)\,\bar{F}(q,p_{1},p_{4})=-4\,g\,\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,p_{2}\cdot\theta\,p_{3}\big)\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,p_{1}\cdot\theta\,p_{4}\big)\ ,\end{split}

(3.60)

and we suppressed the overall delta-functions enforcing momentum conservation.
These satisfy the off-shell kinematic Jacobi identity
n_{s}+n_{t}+n_{u}=0
for any deformation parameter
t
, as a consequence of the Jacobi identity for the infinite-dimensional Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)
. This mirrors the Jacobi identity
c_{s}+c_{u}+c_{t}=0
satisfied by the colour numerators from
eq.

2.27
.

Colour-kinematics duality.

Using these perturbative computations we show colour-kinematics duality directly from the
L_{\infty}
-recursion relations.
We use the evaluation map
\mathrm{ev}:\mathrm{Sh}(i,n-1)\longrightarrow\mathscr{W}_{i}\otimes\mathscr{W}_{n-i}
from
eq.

A.52
, into the tensor product of ordered words, to translate the recursion relations into the language of binary trees
[
46
]
.

For the adjoint scalar theory, the Berends–Giele currents (
3.54
) and minimal model brackets (
3.56
) can be written as a sum over deconcatenations of the ordered word
w=1\cdots n
into non-empty ordered words
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
:

\begin{split}J(w)&=\frac{g}{2}\,\sum_{w=w_{1}\sqcup w_{2}}\,G_{\rm F}\circ\llbracket J(w_{1}),J(w_{2})\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\mu_{n}^{\circ\textrm{\tiny AS}}\big(\phi(1),\ldots,\phi(n)\big)&=-\frac{g}{2}\,\sum_{w=w_{1}\sqcup w_{2}}\,{\sf p}\circ\llbracket J(w_{1}),J(w_{2})\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}\ \mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p_{1\cdots n}\cdot x}\ .\end{split}

(3.61)

We have observed in
eq.

3.41
that the bracket
\llbracket-,-\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}
of the adjoint scalar theory has a tensor product factorisation into the Lie bracket on the colour Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and the Lie bracket bracket on the kinematic Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)
, as illustrated by the coefficient of the three-point vertex (
3.45
).

Given a word
w=k_{1}\cdots k_{n}\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
labelling
n
external particles, we denote its length by
|w|=n
and factorise the symmetric bracket into the tensor product of maps,

\begin{split}C:\mathscr{W}_{n}\xrightarrow{\ \ell_{\rm c}\ }(\mathscr{L}_{n},[-,-]_{\mathfrak{g}})\xrightarrow{\ {\rm col}\ }\mathfrak{g}\quad,\quad K:\mathscr{W}_{n}\xrightarrow{\ \ell_{\rm k}\ }(\mathscr{L}_{n},[-\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}-]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)})\xrightarrow{\ {\rm kin}\ }\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)\ ,\end{split}

(3.62)

as
\llbracket-,-\rrbracket_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}(w)=\big({\rm col}\circ\ell_{\rm c}(w)\big)\otimes\big({\rm kin}\circ\ell_{\rm k}(w)\big)
.
We have made explicit the left bracketing maps
\ell_{\rm c}
and
\ell_{\rm k}
from
eq.

A.45
for colour and kinematical numerators into the multilinear Lie polynomials with colour and kinematical Lie brackets, respectively.
In components we find

\begin{split}C(w)&=[\cdots[[T^{a_{k_{1}}},T^{a_{k_{2}}}]_{\mathfrak{g}},T^{a_{k_{3}}}]_{\mathfrak{g}},\ldots,T^{a_{k_{n}}}]_{\mathfrak{g}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
K(w)&=[\cdots[[\phi^{a_{k_{1}}}_{k_{1}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\phi^{a_{k_{2}}}_{k_{2}}]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\phi^{a_{k_{3}}}_{k_{3}}]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\ldots\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\phi^{a_{k_{n}}}_{k_{n}}]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\ .\end{split}

(3.63)

Finally, one arrives at

J(w)=-\frac{1}{2}\,\sum_{w=w_{1}\sqcup w_{2}}\,\frac{(\,\mathrm{i}\,g)^{|w|-1}}{s_{w}}\,C(w)\otimes K(w)\ ,

(3.64)

with recursion relations for the colour and kinematic numerators given by

\begin{split}C(w)&={\rm col}\circ\sum_{w=w_{1}\sqcup w_{2}}\,[\ell_{\rm c}(w_{1}),\ell_{\rm c}(w_{2})]_{\mathfrak{g}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
K(w)&={\rm kin}\circ\sum_{w=w_{1}\sqcup w_{2}}\,[\ell_{\rm k}(w_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\ell_{\rm k}(w_{2})]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\ .\end{split}

(3.65)

One can replace the recursion (
3.64
) in terms of the binary tree map from
eq.

A.51
by defining
[C\otimes K]\circ w:=C(w)\otimes K(w)
for any word
w
, which yields

\displaystyle J(w)=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,(\,\mathrm{i}\,g)^{|w|-1}\,[C\otimes K]\circ b(w)\ .

(3.66)

One obtains the tree-level amplitude with partial ordering
1w\in\mathscr{W}_{n}
by simply cancelling the overall pole in the current
J(w)
.
Indeed, from
eq.

3.58
, cyclicity of the inner product on the minimal model implies that there is only one such component, namely

\mathcal{M}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{n}(1,w)=-\mathrm{i}\,s_{1w}\,J(w)\ (2\pi)^{d}\,\delta(p_{1w})\ ,

(3.67)

where the overall momentum conservation comes from integrating over spacetime.
Notice that the Moyal–Weyl product is trivialised by momentum conservation.

The full
n
-point amplitude, obtained by summing over all ordered words, can then be written as

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{n}(p,a)=\sum_{{\sf\Gamma}\,\in\,\mathscr{T}_{3,n}}\,\frac{c_{\sf\Gamma}\,n_{\sf\Gamma}}{D_{\sf\Gamma}}\ ,

(3.68)

generalising
eq.

3.59
. Here
\mathscr{T}_{3,n}
denotes the set of all trivalent trees with
n
external edges; it has cardinality
(2n-5)!!
. Associated to any tree
{\sf\Gamma}\in\mathscr{T}_{3,n}
there is a denominator
D_{\sf\Gamma}=\prod_{e\in{\sf\Gamma}}\,s_{e}
with propagators
s_{e}
assigned to each internal edge
e
of
{\sf\Gamma}
. The colour factors
c_{\sf\Gamma}
are contractions of the structure constants of the colour Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
associated to
{\sf\Gamma}
, while
n_{\sf\Gamma}
are the kinematic parts of the numerators involving analogous contractions of the structure constants of the kinematic Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)
.

We conclude that, since the colour algebra based on
\mathfrak{g}
naturally obeys the generalised Jacobi identities, or equivalently the STU-relations on any Jacobi subgraph, the kinematic numerators also obey them.
This is an instance of colour-kinematics duality: the infinite-dimensional kinematic Lie algebra
\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)
which determines the kinematic numerators is dual to the colour Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
. Moreover, from the perspective of
eq.

3.66
, the effect of the zeroth copy construction is to replace the kinematic numerators
K(w)
with a second set of colour numerators
\bar{C}(w)
.

The colour-kinematics duality here is in fact implied by the factorisation of the three-point vertex (
3.45
) into
f^{abc}\,\bar{F}(k,p,q)
and holds off-shell, as is evident from the homotopy algebraic perspective. At loop-level all integrands are computed using
eq.

3.45
, and hence all loop-level kinematic Jacobi identities are automatically satisfied, even off-shell. Below we shall construct the action functional of the double copied theory using Maurer–Cartan theory.

3.6
Homotopy double copy

Our discussion of colour-kinematics duality in §
3.5
justifies the use of the double copy prescription. The factorisation of the Berends–Giele currents in
eq.

3.66
, with respect to the (untwisted) tensor product
\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)
of Lie algebras, is a manifestation of the factorisation (
3.50
) of the strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{AS}
. This permits us to exploit the powerful techniques of
L_{\infty}
-algebras: the homotopy double copy construction
[
12
]
replaces the colour factor
\mathfrak{g}
in the factorization (
3.50
) of
\mathfrak{AS}
with another copy of the “twisted” kinematic factor
\mathfrak{Kin}_{\wedge^{0}}=\mathbbm{R}
, producing the cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathfrak{AS}}=\mathfrak{Kin}_{\wedge^{0}}\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\bar{\star}}(1)}}(\mathfrak{Kin}_{\wedge^{0}}\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}}\mathfrak{Scal})

(3.69)

as a twisted tensor product between the graded vector space
\mathfrak{Kin}_{\wedge^{0}}
(concentrated in degree
0
) and the
C_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}=\mathfrak{Kin}_{\wedge^{0}}\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}}\mathfrak{Scal}
.

The underlying graded vector space is identified as
\mathbbm{R}\otimes(\mathbbm{R}\otimes L)\simeq L
, and the twisted differential is
\widehat{\mu}_{1}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}=m_{1}^{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}}=\square
, so that again the cochain complex
\mathsf{Ch}(\widehat{\mathfrak{AS}}
) is the cochain complex
\mathsf{Ch}_{\rm Scal}
from
eq.

2.4
. The bracket of the double copy is given by the doubly twisted bracket

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\mu}_{2}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})&=\mu_{2}^{\rm Scal}\circ 2\,\mathrm{i}\,\sin\big(\tfrac{\bar{t}}{2}\,\bar{\theta}^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\otimes\partial_{\nu}\big)\circ 2\,\mathrm{i}\,\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,\theta^{\lambda\rho}\,\partial_{\lambda}\otimes\partial_{\rho}\big)(\phi_{1}\otimes\phi_{2})\ ,\end{split}

(3.70)

where we use two independent expansion parameters
t,\bar{t}\in\mathbbm{R}
to keep track of the double Moyal–Weyl deformation, determined by star-products
\star,\bar{\star}
which quantize two generally independent constant Poisson bivectors
\theta,\bar{\theta}
on
\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1}
.
Note that this is symmetric in
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
, as required for a strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra bracket.
It may be written explicitly as a formal power series

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widehat{\mu}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{2}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})\\[4.0pt]
&\hskip 28.45274pt=\mathrm{i}\,\kappa\,\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\frac{(-1)^{n}\,\bar{t}^{\,2n+1}}{(2n+1)!}\,\bar{\theta}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\cdots\bar{\theta}^{\mu_{2n+1}\nu_{2n+1}}\,[\partial_{\mu_{1}}\cdots\partial_{\mu_{2n+1}}\phi_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\partial_{\nu_{1}}\cdots\partial_{\nu_{2n+1}}\phi_{2}]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\ ,\end{split}

(3.71)

where we denote the coupling as
g=\frac{\kappa}{2}
in the double copied theory, as it acquires different engineering dimension.

The Maurer–Cartan equation associated to
\widehat{\mathfrak{AS}}
yields the field equation which governs the dynamics of the double copy as

\displaystyle\square\,\phi+\frac{\mathrm{i}\,\kappa}{2}\,\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\frac{(-1)^{n}\,\bar{t}^{\,2n+1}}{(2n+1)!}\,\bar{\theta}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\cdots\bar{\theta}^{\mu_{2n+1}\nu_{2n+1}}\,[\partial_{\mu_{1}}\cdots\partial_{\mu_{2n+1}}\phi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\partial_{\nu_{1}}\cdots\partial_{\nu_{2n+1}}\phi]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}=0\ .

(3.72)

It can be derived as the stationary locus of the Maurer–Cartan functional, which using the cyclic inner product of
\mathfrak{Scal}
from
eq.

2.7
reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{S}_{\textrm{\tiny AS}}[\phi]=\int\,\mathrm{d}^{d}x\ &\frac{1}{2}\,\phi\star\square\,\phi-\frac{\mathrm{i}\,\kappa}{6}\,\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\,\frac{(-1)^{n}\,\bar{t}^{\,2n+1}}{(2n+1)!}\,\bar{\theta}^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\cdots\bar{\theta}^{\mu_{2n+1}\nu_{2n+1}}\\
&\hskip 142.26378pt\times\phi\,[\partial_{\mu_{1}}\cdots\partial_{\mu_{2n+1}}\phi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\star}}{{,}}\partial_{\nu_{1}}\cdots\partial_{\nu_{2n+1}}\phi]_{\mathfrak{u}(1)}\ .\end{split}

(3.73)

The three-point Feynman vertex following from (
3.73
) is

\begin{split}\hbox to231.82pt{\vbox to54.38pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 27.66254pt\lower-27.18808pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -16.07 -21.26 L -5.94 -12.76}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.76604}{0.6428}{-0.6428}{0.76604}{-4.13649pt}{-9.09108pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -2.88 3.32 C -2.35 1.33 -1.18 0.39 0 0 C -1.18 -0.39 -2.35 -1.33 -2.88 -3.32}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{9.44505pt}{8.933pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}\normalsize{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{50.13364pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{split}

(3.74)

where the kinematic structure constants are given by
eq.

3.7
.

Remark 3.75
.

This scalar theory is a double noncommutative deformation of the cubic scalar field theory of §
2.1
. It can be obtained from the adjoint scalar theory by performing substitutions analogous to
eq.

3.47
for the remaining colour factor, or equivalently by the substitutions

\displaystyle\phi^{a\bar{a}}\longrightarrow\phi\ ,\quad f^{abc}\,\bar{f}^{\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{c}}\longrightarrow F(k,p,q)\,\bar{F}(k,p,q)\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\mathrm{Tr}_{\mathfrak{g}\otimes\bar{\mathfrak{g}}}\longrightarrow\textrm{\small$\int$}

(3.76)

directly in the Lagrangian of the commutative biadjoint scalar theory of §
2.2
.
Hence the double copy of the adjoint scalar theory can in this sense be regarded as a biadjoint scalar theory based on the twisted tensor product
\mathfrak{u}(1)\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(1)}}\mathfrak{u}_{\bar{\star}}(1)
of Lie algebras.

Perturbative calculations.

To get the Berends–Giele currents and
n
-point amplitudes of the double copy, one simply needs to perform calculations analogous to those presented for the adjoint scalar in §
3.5
.
The double copied homotopy algebra is a strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra with bracket
\widehat{\mu}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{2}
and contracting homotopy
\widehat{{\sf h}}=\widehat{G}_{\rm F}
satisfying

(\widehat{\mu}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{1}\circ\widehat{G}_{\rm F})(x,y)=\mathrm{i}\,\delta(x-y)\qquad\mbox{with}\quad\widehat{G}_{\rm F}(\mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p\cdot x})=-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{p^{2}}\,\mathrm{e}^{\,\mathrm{i}\,p\cdot x}\ .

(3.77)

This is just the scalar contracting homotopy acting on the double copied fields, which are scalars.

For example, the tree-level off-shell four-point amplitude is found in this way to be

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{A}}^{\,\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{4}(p)=\sum_{\sigma\in\mathrm{Sh}(1;3)}\,\widehat{\mathcal{M}}^{\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{4}\big(1,\sigma(2),\sigma(3),\sigma(4)\big)=\frac{n_{s}\,\bar{n}_{s}}{s}+\frac{n_{t}\,\bar{n}_{t}}{t}+\frac{n_{u}\,\bar{n}_{u}}{u}\ ,

(3.78)

as expected from the replacement rule (
3.76
). More generally, the
n
-point amplitudes follow by replacing the colour factors
C(w)
with a second set of kinematic numerators
\bar{K}(w)
in
eq.

3.66
and can be written in the form

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{A}}^{\,\textrm{\tiny AS}}_{n}(p)=\sum_{{\sf\Gamma}\,\in\,\mathscr{T}_{3,n}}\,\frac{n_{\sf\Gamma}\,\bar{n}_{\sf\Gamma}}{D_{\sf\Gamma}}\ .

(3.79)

3.7
Binoncommutative biadjoint scalar theory

The homotopy double copy construction of §
3.6
inspires a more general class of noncommutative deformations of the biadjoint scalar theory from §
2.2
. They are obtained from the noncommutative biadjoint scalar theory of
section

3.2
by replacing the tensor product
\mathfrak{g}\otimes\mathfrak{u}_{\bar{\star}}(\bar{N})
with the twisted tensor product
\mathfrak{u}(N)\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(N)}}\mathfrak{u}_{\bar{\star}}(\bar{N})
. The double copy theory of §
3.6
is then recovered in the rank one limits
N=\bar{N}=1
. This ‘binoncommutative’ biadjoint scalar theory will turn out to be the zeroth copy theory for noncommutative gauge theories. In particular, it will play an important role when we study the KLT relations in association with noncommutative Yang–Mills theory in §
5
.

The binoncommutative biadjoint scalar theory is organised by a cyclic strict
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star\bar{\star}}
which is given by the twisted tensor product construction of
section

3.3
, now applied to both Lie algebra factors:

\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star\bar{\star}}:=\mathfrak{u}(N)\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\star}(N)}}\big(\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\otimes_{\tau^{\mathfrak{u}_{\bar{\star}}(\bar{N})}}\mathfrak{Scal}\big)\ .

(3.80)

The underlying cochain complex
\mathsf{Ch}(\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star\bar{\star}})
is given by
eq.

2.12
with
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{u}(N)
and
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})
, which as usual identifies the differential as
\mu_{1}^{\star\bar{\star}\textrm{\tiny BAS}}=\mu_{1}^{\textrm{\tiny BAS}}=\square
. The higher bracket
\mu_{2}^{\star\bar{\star}\textrm{\tiny BAS}}
is the binoncommutative product

\begin{split}\mu_{2}^{\star\bar{\star}\textrm{\tiny BAS}}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})&=T^{c}\otimes\bar{T}^{\bar{c}}\otimes\mu_{2}^{\rm Scal}\circ\Big(f^{abc}\cos\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,\theta^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\otimes\partial_{\nu}\big)-d^{abc}\sin\big(\tfrac{t}{2}\,\theta^{\mu\nu}\,\partial_{\mu}\otimes\partial_{\nu}\big)\Big)\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\circ\Big(\bar{f}^{\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{c}}\cos\big(\tfrac{\bar{t}}{2}\,\bar{\theta}^{\lambda\rho}\,\partial_{\lambda}\otimes\partial_{\rho}\big)-\,\bar{d}^{\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{c}}\sin\big(\tfrac{\bar{t}}{2}\,\bar{\theta}^{\lambda\rho}\,\partial_{\lambda}\otimes\partial_{\rho}\big)\Big)\big(\phi_{1}^{a\bar{a}}\otimes\phi_{2}^{b\bar{b}}\big)\end{split}

(3.81)

of elements
\phi_{1}=T^{a}\otimes\bar{T}^{\bar{a}}\otimes\phi_{1}^{a\bar{a}}
and
\phi_{2}=T^{b}\otimes\bar{T}^{\bar{b}}\otimes\phi_{2}^{b\bar{b}}
in
\Omega^{0}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{u}(N)\otimes\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\big)[-1]
. The cyclic inner product is again identified as the pairing (
2.16
) of fields
\phi\in\Omega^{0}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{u}(N)\otimes\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\big)[-1]
with antifields
\phi^{+}\in\Omega^{0}\big(\mathbbm{R}^{1,d-1},\mathfrak{u}(N)\otimes\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})\big)[-2]
.

One can expand the bracket (
3.81
) analogously to what we did in §
3.6
, and formally develop the Maurer–Cartan theory underlying the cyclic
L_{\infty}
-algebra
\mathfrak{BAS}_{\star\bar{\star}}
. Similarly to the noncommutative biadjoint scalar theory of §
3.2
, this theory has a rigid symmetry under the adjoint action of
\mathfrak{u}(N)\oplus\mathfrak{u}(\bar{N})
and can be extended to a four-parameter family of binoncommutative biadjoint scalar theories with structure constants
\lambda^{abc}_{u,v}
and
\bar{\lambda}^{\bar{a}\bar{b}\bar{c}}_{\bar{u},\bar{v}}
(cf. Remark
3.27
).
The resulting formulas are even more complicated than those of §
3.6
, and they will not be needed explicitly in this paper.

In momentum space, this theory has a remarkably much simpler representation, giving the three-point vertex in its Feynman diagram expansion in terms of two copies of the structure constants from
eq.

3.17
as

\begin{split}\hbox to281.65pt{\vbox to80.5pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 50.05519pt\lower-40.53566pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{}}{}{{}}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{11.43686pt}{9.50209pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
